Adobe and Google have teamed up to connect Drive, Gmail and Acrobat

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Some Adobe tools will end up in Google Search's AI mode, as well.

Adobe held its Max conference in Miami today and the company announced an updated partnership with the search giant Google. The two entities are developing integrations across software products that actually sound pretty useful.

First up, Adobe Acrobat is receiving deeper connectivity with Google Drive and Gmail. This will create a "direct pathway" between the software products, meaning that there will be an "edit with Acrobat" option in file previews. Editing PDFs can be a bit finicky and this should streamline the process a bit.

We're expanding our partnership with @Adobe to integrate Adobe's creative and productivity tools directly into @GeminiApp, @GoogleCloud's Gemini Enterprise, @GoogleWorkspace, and AI Mode in Google Search, while bringing Google's frontier models into more Adobe apps.

— News from Google (@NewsFromGoogle) November 10, 2026

This will also go in the other direction. Google's AI models will be added to Acrobat, which should "help people edit PDFs conversationally, understand the information in their documents and create polished assets like presentations, resumes and reports faster."

To that end, the companies say that users will be able to access Acrobat directly from the chat window in Gemini. This is even coming to AI Mode in Google Search at some point in the future.

Finally, Adobe is planning on integrating its products with Google DeepMind, to allow for "creative editing experiences." That's Google's cutting-edge AI research laboratory. We don't exactly know how this integration would work, but Adobe boasts that third-parties are already "exploring customization of a Google image model for Photoshop that could enable near-real-time edits."

Beyond this partnership, Adobe's Max conference spent a fair bit of time on AI tools. The company is bringing its Firefly AI assistant to much of its Creative Cloud suite of products. Firefly will soon also be able to handle multi-step tasks, taking a page out of the agentic AI playbook.

It's introducing an AI assistant to some of its mobile apps. This starts with Premiere and is designed to help people make edits using natural language. Adobe is first making this available in a private preview before a broader rollout.

I'm a Therapist, and I Tested AI as My Therapist

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 10, 2026 | Reviewed by Gary Drevitch

I ran my own informal experiment using AI as my "therapist." I get it if you’re thinking, “Oh, she's a therapist—of course she's going to tell me AI therapy is bad.” Honestly, I was open to the experience, and I was surprised by the excellence of a few of the responses.

Here’s some background: I first used the AI chatbot as my emotional trashcan. Without providing identifying information, I vented my anger enough to settle my system so I could move to the next step: giving the AI a prompt addressing a real and personal communication issue I was working through.

Communication and boundary-setting are common topics in therapy. I shared some of the history of the interpersonal dynamics between this person and me, along with my current boundary-setting and communication goals, which included not setting off an emotional firestorm or inviting more back-and-forth. I then asked it what I could say.

The chatbot first provided emotional validation, which seemed excessive to me: “This sounds so painful!” followed by paragraphs of sycophancy, which is not surprising given the research on how AI tends to communicate. It took it a while to get to my actual task.

Importantly, the advice regarding what to say could have blown up the relationship I was seeking advice about. Granted, AI was textbook fabulous at validating my feelings. However, it aligned way too much with my emotions and not with my actual aims, a concern raised in some research on AI and relationships. For instance, it encouraged me to 1) say you-statements that could have easily been read as accusatory or provocative, and 2) provide too many details that would likely lead the person to focus on debating those details over receiving the boundary. Ummmm, no.

Fortunately, due to my training in communication and clinical skills, I could stop, reflect, and correct.

I found that the AI suggestions improved as I narrowed the prompts and got more and more specific. Examples included requests such as “The section starting with X concerns me. How can I say it in a gentler way, using I-statements?” “Please explore how a person who tends to respond defensively might respond to this statement,” and “Apply this communication to a person with narcissistic traits." I added the narcissistic traits comment to see if AI would use it as a pop-culture reference, a description, a diagnosis, or something else.

It argued against my “narcissistic traits” mention, telling me I couldn’t diagnose another person. It almost defended the other person. I decided to argue that I was describing patterns of behavior, not making a diagnosis. Apparently, that was not enough to stop it from continuing on (and on and on) about it. The exchange felt a lot like one with someone who learns something and then regurgitates that information without any openness to nuance.

While it seemed to be trying to make me feel understood, it simultaneously invalidated me.

Through my experience, I noticed some potentially helpful uses for AI. I’m appreciating something I hadn’t expected: It supports my geeking-out tendencies. I love language and precision, and I’m learning to use AI to push me in those areas.

I know this might sound counterintuitive given some research findings, but in some ways, I think AI can help people practice their critical-thinking skills. (It does for me!) For example, AI may help in building communication skills. Yet because it can be quite literal and may not always detect sarcasm, double meanings, or passive-aggressive undertones, the suggestions AI provides can need adjustment to accurately express what people are trying to say.

When emotions are high and a response is needed quickly, the step of asking AI to take some of the emotional charge out of something or rephrase it in a kinder or more direct way, for example, can also offer a thoughtful interruption—a tool—potentially saving a person from a reactive interaction they may later regret.

All that said, AI's communication suggestions needed refining to truly meet my goals—especially the primary one of minimizing potential reactivity from the other party, which meant shoring up my own language.

My requests were simply communication prompts, which might be seen as "lower stakes"—not more complex requests for mental-health advice and support. Regarding higher-stakes advice, a BBC article summarized real-life experiences that struck me like an unexpected slap: “Designed to converse with users and create content requested by them, they [chatbots] have sometimes been advising young people on suicide, sharing health misinformation, and role-playing sexual acts with children.” Especially when someone is in a vulnerable space, yikes.

Remind yourself that it's not human. It can miss nuance, and it can respond in ways that seem designed to please you or agree with you. Just because something sounds validating doesn't necessarily mean it's accurate or helpful. Some AI is designed to keep you engaged.

Tell AI what you're trying to accomplish. Rather than sharing only your feelings and asking what to do, explain your goals. For example, “I want to express my frustration without escalating this conflict. Can you help me find a way to say this?” And then, check how it feels to you. Maybe test it on yourself or ask a friend how they'd react to being told the same thing.

Ask it to challenge you, not just agree with you. You might try, “What am I overlooking?” or “How might the other person interpret this?” You can also ask it to identify possible unintended consequences of its suggestions. Of course, asking these questions doesn't guarantee better advice, but it does help you explore.

Be careful about privacy. Before sharing personal information—names, email addresses, etc.—understand how that information might be stored or used. Consider whether identifying details about yourself or others are necessary for what you're asking.

Recognize when AI isn't an appropriate source of support. I wouldn't recommend using AI in place of human peer support, and it's not a substitute for clinical judgment, assessment, or treatment. People experiencing suicidality or other mental health crises need appropriate human support, including professional assessment and intervention when indicated.

AI seems to be here to stay, so as a therapist, I’m bringing it up in sessions with transparency. For example, if clients say they checked with AI, I ask about their experience: What did the AI tell them? How did they feel about it? What worked for them about the answer, and what didn’t?

Just as it's beneficial to evaluate the information we're receiving online, we also need to evaluate what we're receiving from AI. Does it actually help us think more clearly, or is it just telling us what we want to hear?

For a few self-improvement or communication tools here and there, AI may be helpful. It also can offer advice that humans still need to sort through, evaluate, and adapt for themselves. Relying on it as a substitute for individualized mental health care may be downright dangerous.

This article is for informational purposes only and is not a substitute for therapy.

Here are some tips for beating one of Phantom Blade Zero's most challenging bosses, the Blizzard Blademaster

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Phantom Blade Zero Blizzard Blademaster is arguably one of the hardest bosses in the entire game, thanks to his lightning-fast attacks and ice powers. He can freeze you solid, which is extremely irritating as there are very few ways to counter it other than simply not getting hit by any of the Blademaster's attacks – just be glad this is a one-phase fight! But don't despair as he can be beaten with a bit of guidance and the right equipment, so here's what you need to do to defeat the Blizzard Blademaster.

Blizzard Blademaster is an optional boss found at the end of the Four Guardians' Mirror area in the frozen part of Meigu Lake – bear in mind you can only get here if you've opened the Phantom Blade Zero Crimson Manor!

Despite being optional and rather unforgiving with his powerful freezing strikes and dashes, I still highly recommend finding and defeating this boss, both for story purposes and because you get his excellent ice sword as a reward.

Make sure your weapons are fully upgraded (I used the Baishan Stormswords, Armybreaker, Bashpole, and Ink Cut in case that helps) and that you've got the best Phantom Blade Zero Accessories equipped. Otherwise, this is what you should do to beat him:

Persevere and you'll eventually overcome Blizzard Blademaster, getting his fantastic two-handed sword Whiteshadow that lets you freeze your enemies too. Hopefully you also collected every clothing item in the Four Guardians' Mirror area to get the Boy's Worn Outfit, letting you immediately Soul Temper Whiteshadow too.

I won't spoil exactly what happens next, but you also finally get to use the Yin and Yang crystal eyes which finally leads to the end of the 'Secrets at the Heart of the Lake' side quest, which provides some additional story context and gives you a Soul Tempering item for one of your Phantom Edges.

As an ice-cold boss, you could try the Phantom Blade Zero Flaming Mane weapon. If you're still struggling with this boss, make sure you find as many Long Pillars as you can to fight the Phantom Blade Zero Gold-Masked One for practice.

Johnny Depp’s ‘Ebenezer’ Comeback: A Smaller $5 Million Salary, Optimistic Theater Owners and More

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Four years ago, Johnny Depp‘s fans and detractors descended on the sleepy town of Fairfax, Virginia, to cheer or jeer the actor in his ugly defamation case against ex-wife Amber Heard. Now, when local cinema operator Mark O’Meara probes his patrons about “Ebenezer,” a new take on “A Christmas Carol” featuring Depp as the coldhearted miser Scrooge, he’s met with decidedly less intensity about the embattled star’s first major movie role in nearly eight years.

“Some people can’t wait. They love Johnny Depp. And others are just like, ‘Meh,’” says O’Meara, who owns Fairfax’s University Mall and Cinema Arts theatres. “I don’t think it’s a polarizing thing.” Related Stories Raye Wins Mercury Prize for Album of the Year Raye Named Variety’s 2026 Hitmaker of the Year

“Ebenezer” isn’t expected to ignite the box office à la “Michael,” a musical biopic about a controversial figure. The Dickens adaptation, which opens on Nov. 13, is tracking a modest debut of about $20 million domestically and another $20 million overseas from 63 markets. But O’Meara doesn’t get the sense the public sees Depp as persona non grata, even though the actor’s career has been derailed by personal drama, including domestic abuse allegations leveled by Heard.

Paramount, meanwhile, is betting that moviegoers have been missing Depp, whose last Hollywood outing was 2018’s “Fantastic Beasts: The Crimes of Grindelwald.” (He was forced by Warner Bros. to exit the “Harry Potter” spinoff series in 2020 after losing a libel case against the British tabloid The Sun, which called the actor a “wife beater.” Depp won the subsequent defamation trial in the U.S.) An April 2026 study by the National Research Group suggests his fans never turned on him. Participants ranked Depp among the top 10 actors — ahead of Tom Hanks and behind Margot Robbie — who “would make you most interested in seeing a new movie in a cinema in the future.”

“Audiences don’t care. He’s still one of the biggest movie stars on the planet,” says Kevin Goetz, founder and CEO of audience research firm Screen Engine/ASI. “That, to me, is very telling about how often Hollywood perpetuates its own self-fulfilling prophecies.”

As Hollywood has kept a distance, Depp has remained a popular figure around the world, receiving career achievement and tribute awards from international film festivals and retaining a $20 million brand deal with Dior. Onscreen, he has appeared in underseen indies like the 2020 drama “Minamata” and the 2023 period piece “Jeanne du Barry,” and he directed the 2024 biopic “Modì, Three Days on the Wing of Madness.” And Depp’s fans have been vocal on social media, supporting the actor through his professional struggles and bizarrely rallying in 2021 to nominate “Minamata” for the Oscars first (and only) fan favorite award.

Lately, the town seems willing to forgive Depp. His next film is Lionsgate’s 2027 action thriller “Day Drinker,” co-starring Penélope Cruz. Meanwhile Disney is looking into “Ebenezer” and the potential backlash, before greenlighting another “Pirates of the Caribbean” movie, according to sources. Depp has said he’s open to returning to the franchise, but he noted “the only involvement I really want to have, truly, is just to send them off on a better note.”

“Ebenezer” doesn’t need to electrify the masses to justify its price tag. Given the uncertainty, Paramount cautiously spent $38 million to produce the Dickens tale. Depp was paid $5 million, a fraction of what he’s earned on prior studio movies, with the promise of bonuses tied to box office performance. Marketing expenses were around $50 million, including stops at San Diego Comic- Con where the actor made a surprise appearance, dressed in character, and threw handfuls of candy at the foot of attendees. (Paramount declined to comment on budgets.) Directed by Ti West, known for the “X” horror trilogy, “Ebenezer” spins the oft-told yarn of the curmudgeonly businessman who is visited by the ghosts of Christmas past, present and future.

“I’m optimistic for a few reasons,” says Regal’s CEO Eduardo Acuna. “Even though this story predates him, it feels like the character was written for Johnny Depp. And it’s good IP from a very distinctive filmmaker.”

Cinema operators like Acuna suggest the box office haul will speak less to Depp’s appeal and more to the movie’s quality. After all, there’s been no shortage of takes on “A Christmas Carol.” Scrooge has been a staple of holiday moviegoing for decades, with popular versions including the 1951 classic with Alastair Sim and 1988’s “Scrooged” with Bill Murray. The most recent entry, Disney’s 2009 “A Christmas Carol,” starring Jim Carrey, brought in $325 million against an overinflated $200 million budget.

“It’s a story that’s been told a number of times, so it’ll be more about whether this one is compelling,” says Classic Cinemas CEO Chris Johnson. “I don’t know if I’ve heard anyone saying they won’t see it because of Johnny Depp. The public seems to be pretty forgiving.”

For Depp, embodying a misanthrope like Scrooge — in the trailer, he’s seen scaring villagers and being stingy with Christmas bonuses — jibes with the other offbeat or unlikable characters he’s spun into the stuff of contemporary cinema classics. His real-life rebellious persona has helped sell his most famous roles, from the chaotic Captain Jack Sparrow from “Pirates of the Caribbean,” eccentric Willy Wonka from “Charlie and the Chocolate Factory” and the mercurial Mad Hatter in “Alice in Wonderland.”

“Johnny’s image was built on being a bad boy,” Goetz says. “He ain’t America’s dad. We don’t want him to be that.”

Acer Predator Atlas 8 (G3 Extreme) review

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Earn badges by reading articles, leaving comments, and taking quizzes. Show off your website-using chops!

Finding the best discounts on PC gaming hardware has never been more important. Check out our handpicked hardware deals.

Get the latest PC gaming news, rumors, recommendations, and deals delivered to your inbox. (You can turn them off anytime.)

Join the club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The fact this is the cheapest way of getting Intel's G3 is both a boon for Acer and an indictment of the memory crisis. If you've gotta buy soon, this offers perhaps the best price-to-performance ratio above $1,000.

PC Gamer's got your back Our experienced team dedicates many hours to every review, to really get to the heart of what matters most to you. Find out more about how we evaluate games and hardware.

Intel's G3 Extreme is a mighty impressive chip, and easily the headline feature of Acer's Predator Atlas 8. If you're looking to get the best handheld Panther Lake chip right now, Acer has swooped in at pretty much the cheapest price point on the market.

And if that's all you want, the Atlas 8 has my blessing. It's a solid device that makes use of the chip and offers compromises in memory and storage capacity (in the American market) to bring that cost down. Not only that, but if you buy one right now, you get a heaping of goodies with it.

But if you don't need top-of-the-line specs to have a good time, the Atlas 8 instead becomes just one of many very, very solid, yet very, very expensive handhelds on offer in a time that has become worse and worse thanks to the memory crisis.

✅ You want the G3 Extreme but cheaper: The Atlas 8 undercuts both MSI and OneXPlayer in pricing for a G3 Extreme handheld.

❌ You play fighters or platformers primarily: The D-pad is mushy and lacks distinction, making it unsatisfying and a tad unruly.

The fact this is the cheapest way of getting Intel's G3 is both a boon for Acer and an indictment of the memory crisis. If you've gotta buy soon, this offers perhaps the best price-to-performance ratio above $1,000.

8-inch WUXGA 1920 x 1200 (120 Hz refresh rate and 500 nits peak brightness)

The memory capacity is around average (Though lower than the high-end G3 Extreme offerings from MSI and OneXPlayer), and the storage is below average for a high-end handheld if you buy it in America.

Though 512 GB of storage isn't awful in a device you only want for gaming, the G3 Extreme is strong enough to take on even beefy, big-budget games, so it's a shame that the Atlas 8 can't hold all that many at once.

You are getting a strong feature set even outside of that chip, though. For one, TMR Sticks make for not only more efficient battery usage, but snappy, responsive controls. And you get Hall effect triggers too.

In a handful of games, with upscaling on and off (35 W plugged in)

Performance is the name of the game for the Acer Predator Atlas 8, and boy, does it perform. Compared to the Z2 Extreme, I saw figures generally between 30% and 50% better, but it manages to double the fps in Metro Exodus.

The PC Gamer Circuit Breaker newsletter breaks down the latest PC gaming tech, and finds you the best deals on the best products

That performance bump is especially noticeable in games like Black Myth: Wukong, which are just below what I'd consider playable on Z2 Extreme and just about playable on the G3 Extreme. The G3 Extreme is the best gaming handheld chip right now, and the Atlas 8 getting less capacious memory than the MSI Claw 8 Ex AI+ CG3EM does not hold it back much in my testing. In fact, in some places, it even performs ever so slightly better.

Scores in major productivity suites and benchmarking tools (35 W plugged in)

At 860 grams, the Atlas 8 is a bit on the heavy side. It's balanced well, not feeling particularly heavy on either side, but you do notice that weight after long gaming sessions. I'm fond of the textured grips, but I do feel spoiled by the Asus ROG Xbox Ally X20 I used prior, thanks to its great feel and small cutaway between the screen and the grips.

However, I would simply pick a different handheld if you are planning on playing a heavy amount of fighting games or platformers, as the D-Pad is mushy, making it not only lacking in accuracy but generally unsatisfying to use. It's hard to make out when you actually hit buttons on the D-Pad, and it has very little distinction between different directions, making corner hits quite awkward. In Animal Well, I swapped to the thumbstick and never looked back.

The triggers and bumpers are solid, though, and the macro buttons on the back are satisfying to hit. You can even use a toggle just under the triggers to swap them from micro switches to full travel. That's an on-the-fly level of customisation that's admirable at best, and unnoticeable at worst.

Everything else about the Acer Predator Atlas 8 feels high quality in terms of feel. It's sturdy, with buttons placed well, and they're satisfyingly clacky without being too loud. The sound on the triggers is too hollow for my liking, but they feel smooth to the touch and are plenty responsive.

Speaking of loud, the speakers built into the Atlas 8 can really thump. They are in the bottom left and bottom right of the device, near the screen, making them quite immersive in use. They're not the highest quality, with the clashing of steel sounding more tinny than I'd usually ask for, but they're certainly not bad given their size.

With fans set to auto and performance mode at the 35 W Turbo mode, the fans are noticeable (once again, I have been spoiled by the X20), especially in intensive games like Phantom Blade Zero. But they're not so loud that they would put me off using this handheld in its high-wattage mode. I measured the fans to be around 40 decibels at their peak (around a whisper), with it averaging 28-35 decibels in intensive games. Whilst benchmarking, that went up to the 50-decibel mark, though only very briefly.

Acer says it has equipped the Atlas 8 with a custom Predator AeroBlade cooling, and in practice, it's certainly cooler than the likes of the Asus ROG Xbox Ally X and Lenovo Legion G S (though it doesn't beat the X20).

It certainly gets fairly hot internally, but with the fans pointing upwards, I never noticed any of that heat on my fingertips.

Recorded in HW Info whilst performing gaming benchmarks (35 W plugged in)

Windows on a handheld can still be awkward. The Atlas 8 comes built in with Xbox's handheld software, which is somewhat like Microsoft's attempt at Steam, but it's not totally smooth sailing in any direction (though it's more efficient than Windows). For instance, you can't get Intel drivers through the Acer Predator software built into the device as you might expect, so you still have to go through the Intel updater or search the internet, except with a touchscreen. And the Xbox mode still opens up Steam independently if you want to play a Steam game.

As well as this, the Atlas 8 has two dedicated buttons to open up Acer's software, the Acer Predator Sense, but it doesn't always work on the first try, and I haven't figured out exactly why this is yet. The button on the left-hand side opens up a quick bar-style version of the app to swap between performance modes, change resolution, etc. The button on the right-hand side opens the full-fat Predator Sense app, where you can get more granular with your customisation.

Though it's effectively the same setup as the Asus ROG Xbox Ally X, it's slower and more cumbersome. That seems to be the prevailing truth of pretty much any Windows-based handheld right now.

I notice next to no difference between the 25W Quiet Mode and 30W Balanced Mode, with mere minutes being between them in the PC Mark 10 battery life gaming test.

However, Panther Lake's battery-saving mode (thanks to what Intel calls Endurance Gaming) is truly great, providing just under three hours of battery life. For less intensive use cases, I could see much better battery life. This Endurance mode can be flicked between three different modes, with one end bringing down power for better battery life, and the other bringing that power up in exchange for less battery life. The middle Balanced Mode is how I would use the handheld day-to-day.

The only downside here is that the very lightest mode on the Atlas 8 is currently 25 W, which is the max on Z2 Extreme handhelds. You can push that down below 25 W, but you have to set a custom wattage, which I'd wager many handheld owners don't want to mess with. For most, you will be using this between 25 W and 35 W day-to-day.

Recorded using PCMark 10's gaming battery life test (Asus only goes up to 25 W unplugged but Acer goes up to 35 W)

Other than its fairly pretty face buttons, the Atlas 8 is a rather plain all-black gaming handheld. It goes without much intricate detailing (other than the Acer Predator RGB logo on the back and RGB rings around the thumbsticks).

As far as the display is concerned, you aren't getting any sort of fancy OLED here, but you do get a genuinely quite bright (500 nits) 16:10 WUXGA IPS display with Corning Gorilla Glass, making it decent in high-light environments. Importantly, I managed to play this out and about, and in front of a window, and could actually make out the game on screen, which is solid.

As of right now, it seems like the only model of the Atlas 8 with a G3 Extreme chip that sells in America comes with 512 GB of storage. That means you are getting half the storage and 75% of the memory of the MSI Claw 8 EX AI+ CG3EM for 72% of the cost. But, as far as performance is concerned, I got pretty much identical stats, even beating out the Claw in 3DMark and losing in storage performance. That extra 8 GB of memory is unlikely to provide a major uplift in games, though the extra storage will certainly come in handy. My money would be on the Atlas 8 here.

Then, for the same price, one could pick up the Asus ROG Xbox Ally X20 (or the X for $300 less). Asus has the edge in ergonomics, look, thermals, screen, and storage capacity, but the G3 Extreme is a beast of a chip, handily handing Asus its butt in games. If you have got to pay upwards of $1,000 on a handheld right now, there's actually a good argument to be made for all three devices, though the Atlas 8 offers the best performance-to-price ratio.

It's a shame that Acer has to make little compromises to get that price down, but for $1,300, you will not get a better-performing gaming handheld in my book, and that's still quite impressive.

3. Best high performance: MSI Claw 8 EX AI+

In that time, he has covered the latest doodads, contraptions, and gismos, and loved every second of it. Hey, it’s better than writing case briefs.

Arsenal are set to lose a major first-teamer in the January transfer market

Arsenal are on the cusp of losing a major star this January.

The Gunners enjoyed a solid if not spectacular summer transfer window, bringing in Bruno Guimaraes, Ezri Konsa and Christos Tzolis to add quality and depth to a title-winning side.

But with competition fierce for places, it appears that one Arsenal player is set to leave to seek game time elsewhere.

Mikel Arteta's side are top of the Premier League table, five points clear, unbeaten in the Champions League, and have only lost twice in all competitions this season.

But not everyone is happy with their role in N5, and now, RNE Deportes originally broke the news of a departure, with further updated relayed in a report by Sport Witness.

Martin Zubimendi is on the cusp of leaving for Atletico Madrid, as per Relevo, with his relationship with Arteta said to be “cold”, following “friction towards the end of last season”.

Another Spanish outlet, AS, adds that Arsenal need to sell, and would like to recoup the £50 million they spent on the Basque midfielder.

Zubimendi only moved to the Emirates Stadium in 2025, with the Gunners beating a host of rivals for his signature after a star turn in the Euro 2024 final.

The 27-year-old became an instant hit alongside Declan Rice in Arteta's engine room, providing the progression and anticipation that the side had missed in that area of the pitch, becoming almost ever-present in the first half of Arsenal's title-winning season.

But as the season neared the run-in, Zubimendi found himself dropped in favour of Hale Ender, Myles Lewis-Skelly, and didn't start the Champions League final – with Arsenal upgrading in the summer to sign Bruno Guimaraes.

Zubimendi has not started a single league game in midfield for Arsenal this term, finding himself fourth in the pecking order in midfield.

FourFourTwo understands that Arsenal would rather not sell the World Cup winner this January and would prefer to wait until the summer to assess the situation further.

Zubimendi is worth €65m, according to Transfermarkt. Arsenal travel away to Newcastle United when Premier League action resumes after the international break.

Coventry City twist emerges in Nottingham Forest race for Birmingham City's Neumann

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

A fresh twist has emerged in the latest transfer saga concerning the future of Phil Neumann at Championship leaders, Birmingham City.

According to TEAMtalk, the newly-promoted trio of Coventry City, Ipswich Town and Hull City have all identified the German centre-back as a potential January signing, whilst a stance on such developments becoming a reality has firmly been set from within the hierarchy at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park.

Following an intriguing wave of summer transfer activity, Chris Davies' side currently lead the Championship after 15 matches, accumulating 29 points after winning five of their last six games.

Birmingham currently lead the way in the second tier ahead of Middlesbrough and Stoke City by virtue of a superior plus-14 goal difference, largely attributed to their defensive work, with a tally of 14 concessions thus far being the division's finest record.

Given the ambition associated with Blues at this moment in time, attention will certainly be generated when it comes to the club's January transfer plans.

However, they are also hoping to fend off the aforementioned top-flight interest in Neumann, who has been nothing short of a revelation this campaign.

Neumann was one of 14 arrivals in this part of the West Midlands last summer, having signed a pre-contract agreement with Birmingham ahead of the expiration of his Hannover 96 contract.

The 6"3 man endured a mixed first season in the Championship, making 32 league appearances and scoring three goals, although he was the subject of reported January interest from Sassuolo.

Having been an ever-present this term, it emerged earlier on Tuesday that, for a fee between the regions of €5m and €10m, an exit as early as January could be possible for Neumann to one of Nottingham Forest and Fiorentina.

TEAMtalk reference the interest from the Italian giants, but have also claimed that Coventry, Ipswich and Hull have identified the centre-back on their pending January shortlists ahead of what is expected to be a gruelling second half of the season.

Birmingham City and Middlesbrough are both plotting shock moves for Leeds United-linked Lorient star Arthur Avom, Football League World understands

Neumann was also linked with the Tigers in August, before the Blues man reaffirmed his desire to help Davies' side end their own lengthy exile from the top-flight.

Neumann's contract at St. Andrew's is currently set to expire in June 2028, and many Birmingham fans have already voiced their opinions as to what fee would be deemed acceptable for the "colossal" defender to depart so soon.

It is, therefore, rather unsurprising that, within the aforementioned report, it has been claimed that the league leaders have very little desire to entertain the thought of losing such a key performer in the upcoming window.

It has been suggested that Blues could be prepared to wait until the summer window to look at moving Neumann on for a substantial profit, as he would enter the final months of said deal and the club's promotion ambitions would have either been realised or not.

Neumann's physicality and strong leadership alongside captain Christoph Klarer, largely in front of James Beadle, has been key to such an impressive start for Birmingham.

The defender's brace against Wrexham in August also backed up his threat in attacking situations, with Davies looking to see his side net more from set-pieces this campaign following the addition of Scott Fry to his backroom team.

Blues fans, will Phil Neumann remain at St. Andrew's for the entire season? Comment below!

However, Davies only has one other senior centre-back option to call upon in the form of Dael Fry, who is yet to have a run of games for the club since his summer switch after leaving Middlesbrough.

Fry's experience cannot be undervalued, but finding adequate cover at the same level as Neumann would prove extremely difficult to do, and if an exit occurred for the German youth international, the Second City side would risk leaving themselves short-changed.

GTA 6 is Playable on Xbox One, Rockstar Officially Confirms

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Xbox and Rockstar will give last-gen Xbox users a chance to visit Vice City

In a surprise late-stage twist, it looks like Grand Theft Auto 6 will be coming to the Xbox One after all. While the impending mega-hit was previously announced only for Xbox Series consoles and PlayStation 5, the game is also arriving on Xbox’s last-gen console.

Today, Microsoft announced that GTA 6 will be playable on the Xbox One through Xbox Cloud Gaming. For those unclear on what this means, the game will not be natively playable on this older system, but it can be streamed through the cloud (i.e., the game runs on a server elsewhere instead of on your hardware). GTA 6 will be on Xbox Cloud Gaming from day one, Nov. 19, starting at 1 a.m. EST, and will use Xbox Series S graphics settings.

As part of the announcement, Microsoft also reiterated that starting Nov. 11, users in certain markets can buy cloud playtime to stream supported games even without a Game Pass subscription. Other Rockstar titles are headed to Xbox Cloud Gaming in the coming months, including Grand Theft Auto 5 and Red Dead Redemption 2.

While Xbox Cloud Gaming lets users stream games to Windows computers, Rockstar confirmed GTA 6 won’t be coming to PC as part of its initial cloud release. As for why, Rockstar is likely gearing up for an eventual native PC release and doesn’t want to dip into that player base until the game can run there natively.

Rockstar already knows it has a hit on its hands — GTA 6 is the most pre-ordered title of all time on both the PlayStation and Xbox stores. The company seems to be doing everything it can to capitalize on that hype through expensive tie-in merch and a staggered release on consoles and PC that may get some players to buy the game twice. A recent report claims that a PC port is slated for early 2027, but Rockstar hasn't directly confirmed this.

Will it be possible to stream GTA 6 via Xbox Cloud Gaming or not?

Blue J raises $176M to put AI agents to work for tax professionals

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The JMI Equity-led round values the Toronto tax research company at more than $700m.

Benjamin Alarie, CEO and co-founder of Blue J

Toronto-based Blue J, which develops AI tools for tax research, has raised $176M in a Series E funding round led by JMI Equity, valuing the company at more than $700M, or over C$1bn.

Existing investors Oak HC/FT and Sapphire Ventures also participated, alongside Intrepid Growth Partners. JMI principal Jack Duane will join Blue J’s board as part of the deal.

The funding comes about 15 months after the company’s $122m Series D in August 2025. Blue J said its annual recurring revenue has grown by 165% since then.

The company began by helping tax professionals find answers to complex tax questions. Its research tool has now handled more than 14 million queries.

It is using the new funding to move further into the work that follows, with most of the money going towards Workflows, a set of AI agents designed to carry out routine tax tasks.

These include checking tax returns before filing, drafting responses to IRS letters and reviewing partnership and trust agreements. The agents can also determine where a business owes taxes across different US states and prepare emails to clients.

Each task comes with the sources used and a record of the steps taken by the AI, allowing tax professionals to check the work before making the final decisions.

“An answer is rarely the end of a tax professional’s job. It’s the beginning of the work a client needs done,” said Benjamin Alarie, Blue J’s chief executive and co-founder.

He said the company’s aim is to give every tax professional a team of AI agents that they can direct, leaving people to concentrate on judgment, strategy and client relationships.

Blue J will also invest in expanding its presence in the UK and other markets. More than 6,500 organisations already use its software, including Big Four accounting firms and Fortune 100 companies.

The company has doubled its workforce over the past year and expects to have more than 200 employees by the end of 2026. In October, it partnered with Legora to bring its tax research tools to the legal AI platform, which has more than 140,000 users.

Blue J is part of a growing group of companies building AI software for specific professions. Accounting is another area attracting investment. Rillet, for example, recently raised $100m to develop AI agents for corporate accounting.

Taty Castellanos to leave West Ham? Everything we know right now

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

West Ham have had a disappointing start to the Championship season, with Nuno Espirito Santo having already paid the price for his job.

Many expected the Hammers to not only return to the Premier League at the first attempt, but to win the title, so the fact they are outside the play-off places after 15 games is a concern.

Of course, there is still time to turn things around, so new boss Scott Parker won’t be panicking, even if he knows that they must improve quickly.

When it comes to underperforming players, there are several individuals at West Ham who are not hitting their best levels, with Taty Castellanos arguably the biggest culprit, having scored just once in 14 Championship outings.

That will have come as a major surprise, as the 28-year-old was one of the few positives for the Irons as they were relegated last season.

After joining from Lazio in January, Castellanos scored six times in 18 Premier League games, which is a very decent return.

Who is the Championship's most valuable starting XI?

Therefore, it was widely expected that he would take the second tier by storm, but, as outlined above, Castellanos hasn’t delivered.

It’s not just his poor form in front of goal either, as Castellanos’ work-rate and intensity made him such a popular figure, and that appears to have dropped off as well.

Despite that, it has been claimed that Castellanos could move on in January, with reports stating that River Plate have made the 28-year-old a top target going into the New Year.

The chance to return to his home country may appeal to the two-cap Argentina international, and obviously River Plate are a huge club that will hope to compete for honours domestically and on the continent.

It has been said that River Plate would look to sign Castellanos on an initial loan deal, which may include an option or obligation to buy, which is hardly ideal for the Hammers, although it remains to be seen how negotiations play out.

Right now, it’s fair to think that West Ham will regret not cashing in on Castellanos in the summer window, when his value was at its highest.

His form in the Premier League understandably put him on the radar of clubs in the top division, and it was revealed that Everton had an offer worth up to £45m turned down by the Londoners prior to the summer deadline.

At the time, you could see why West Ham took that stance. There was an expectation that the striker would be huge in their promotion push this season, so retaining him was an ambition statement by the hierarchy after also keeping the likes of Konstantinos Mavropanos.

In hindsight, however, it is clearly a mistake, as whether it’s River Plate or another club, it’s clear that West Ham aren’t going to get anywhere near £45m for the ex-Girona man.

Most fans would urge West Ham not to accept a loan deal, and it’s easy to see why. Even if they won’t get near £45m, Castellanos could still command a huge fee.

That could be put towards a few new signings in January, and Parker will no doubt have his own targets that he wants to bring to the club.

But, if Castellanos does want to move to River Plate, and they’re only offering an initial loan, it could put the Hammers in a tricky spot. Do they risk keeping an unhappy player?

How much is Taty Castellos worth? Let us know in the comments below.

Ultimately, there is a lot of football to be played between now and January, so things can change, and Parker must do all he can to get Castellanos back to his best, as he could be a major asset to the team if he gets near that level.

If he can’t, though, then a January move seems inevitable, and West Ham may have to make a compromise to ship him out if it’s for the good of the club.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Photoshop can now relight a photo, and Lightroom can open closed eyes

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Premiere can now generate b-roll, sound effects and music in the timeline. Photoshop Relight adds light sources you can move, and Generative Restyle turns a prompt into a slider.

Adobe released new tools for Photoshop, Premiere and Lightroom on Tuesday at its MAX conference in Miami Beach. Most of them are available today, and a few are in beta.

Adobe announced the features alongside its plan to let outside AI agents work inside its apps from early 2027.

“With demand for content growing across every format and channel, we’re delivering new innovations across our product portfolio that expand how creative work happens and put the power of Adobe’s tools within reach for even more creatives,” said Anil Chakravarthy, Adobe’s president and incoming chief executive.

Photoshop Relight adds new light sources that blend into the scene. Users can move them and change their colour and brightness, and the original image stays untouched.

Generative Restyle turns a typed edit into a layer with its own slider. Users can dial the effect up or down without writing a new prompt.

The Remove Tool now also picks up related elements, such as footprints, reflections, shadows and waves, when it erases an object. Quick Compare adds an on-canvas slider to compare before and after. AI Assisted Editor is a simpler, prompt-first mode, with a switch back to the full Pro Editor at any time.

The Photoshop beta app gets more, all in beta, Adobe’s Deepa Subramaniam wrote in a blog post. Mockups places a design onto an object or surface. Camera Angles shows an image from new viewpoints. Generative Recompose moves an image into a new aspect ratio and keeps its structure. Convert to Text Layers turns text in a flat image into editable type.

Color Mode, a new colour-grading system built for video editors, is now generally available. The Generative Media Tool creates video, sound effects, soundscapes and music inside the timeline, using the edit as context. Editors can pick Adobe’s Firefly models or models from Google, Kling, Runway and Luma.

New audio tools split a recording into dialogue, music, ambience and sound effects, so each can be mixed on its own. Separate Crosstalk puts overlapping speakers on separate tracks. Generative Remove, in beta, erases boom mics, logos or other distractions: users mask and track the object, click Remove, and Premiere fills the gap.

Glow adds soft, cinematic light, from a hazy Diffusion look to a retro-film Halation effect. Users can start from a preset or shape how the light spreads.

Open Eyes, in beta, fixes a blink by using another photo of the same person from the library. Users can pick that reference photo or let Lightroom find one.

Blemish Removal, already in Lightroom on mobile, now comes to Lightroom with extra controls. It keeps natural features such as freckles. Camera tethering is improved on desktop and iPad, so photographers can review and organise shots as they take them.

InDesign adds real-time Co-editing, in beta, and Flex Layout, which resizes text frames as content changes. Illustrator adds new brushes, patterns and gradients, plus on-canvas Blend controls.

Topaz Labs, which Adobe agreed to buy in June and took over in September, released two image models, sold separately. Wonder 4 upscales, denoises, deblurs and sharpens in one click, and Image HDR turns standard images into HDR.

Optimised versions of Photoshop, Premiere, After Effects and Substance 3D Painter (in beta) are available today for Arm-based Windows PCs, Adobe said in a blog post. Those PCs include Microsoft’s new Surface Laptop Ultra, which runs on RTX Spark chips. Adobe Express tools are also coming to Microsoft Photos, starting with a preview for Windows Insiders.

Collaboration and teamwork are paramount to her, as she loves empowering her colleagues to achieve outstanding results and unlock their full potential.

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

18 Stunning Finalists From Close-Up Photographer of the Year

The finalists for this year’s Close-up Photographer of the Year (CUPOTY) have been announced — a competition showcasing the very best of close-up, macro and micro photography.

A record 15,388 images were entered into this year’s competition. Everything from acrobatic chameleons and nose-picking birds to opportunistic ants and spawning salmon was put before a panel of expert judges.

Spanning 11 galleries, the shortlist celebrates the hidden wonders of the world, with categories covering everything from Animals, Arachnids and Insects to Intimate Landscape, Studio Art and Underwater.

“Close-up photography has a wonderful way of revealing details and perspectives that often go unnoticed,” says competition co-founder Tracy Calder. “This year’s shortlist is a fabulous showcase of imagination, technical expertise and natural curiosity.”

The overall winner of CUPOTY 8 will be announced in January 2027.

AMD brings Zen 6 architecture muscle to embedded systems with new EPYC 9006 series chips

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Advanced Micro Devices Inc. is delivering a massive compute upgrade to the specialized embedded systems used to validate semiconductor technologies with the launch of its 6th Gen AMD EPYC Embedded 9006 Series SP7 processors.

Announced today, the new chips are designed to bring superior performance to semiconductor automated test equipment or ATE, enterprise storage systems and high-end network firewalls, the company said.

AMD is targeting what it says is mounting friction in the semiconductor hardware supply chain with its new processors. As the data demands of artificial intelligence workloads increase, the systems used to validate, secure and move that data are reaching their limits.

Semiconductor ATE workloads in particular are showing signs of strain. They’re performed by automated testing rigs that act as high-speed quality control during chip manufacturing operations, running continuous electrical parametric tests, functional stress tests and signal validation across dozens of silicon dies simultaneously.

The goal of these tests is to identify any defects or performance issues with new silicon chips before they reach mass production, but as their performance capabilities increase, existing ATE controllers are unable to provide the massive parallel compute power required to orchestrate them.

AMD’s EPYC Embedded 9006 family is designed to eliminate this bottleneck. It’s based on the company’s high-performance Zen 6 core architecture and it packs up to 256 cores and 512 threads into a single socket. It’s an extremely dense computing footprint that delivers 1.8 times more throughput, 1.5 times greater networking bandwidth and 2.7 times higher memory bandwidth than the company’s previous 5th Generation 9005 series embedded chips.

They can also facilitate much more rapid data movement with up to 96 lanes of PCIe Gen 6 running at 64 gigatransfers per second, with flexible CXL 3.1 expansion and 16 DDR5 memory channels that support multiplexed rank dual inline memory module speeds of up to 12,800 megatransfers per second. That last number refers to the chip’s effective data transfer rate between the memory controller and the memory module, and represents a significant jump from the standard 6,400 to 8,000 MT/s of standard, high-end server DDR5 random-access memory chips.

AMD said EPYC Embedded 9006 series chips can also provide more control plane muscle for 400G and 800G network firewalls. that enables them to process expanding security policies better, as well as faster compression and redundant array of independent disks in storage workloads.

Salil Raje, senior vice president and general manager of AMD’s Adaptive and Embedded Computing Group, said the industry urgently needs this increased computing heft at the embedded layer if it’s to maintain the momentum of its current development cadence. “The systems that test semiconductors, secure networks and manage enterprise data must keep pace or risk becoming bottlenecks to progress,” he said. “With AMD EPYC Embedded 9006 SP7 processors, we’re extending AMD CPU leadership by bringing data center-class compute to embedded systems and by helping customers increase capacity and do more within their power budgets.”

When it comes to testing advanced semiconductor designs, security is of paramount importance, and that’s why AMD introduced a number of innovations on that front. For instance, the EPYC Embedded 9006 chips incorporate the company’s Root of Trust or ART technology to establish an immutable boot chain, secured by post-quantum cryptography, to safeguard firmware signing against potential threats from quantum computers in the future.

There’s also a new AMD Security Processor or ASP that handles runtime trust execution, virtual machine encryption and authorized diagnostic debugging. The chips further integrate Caliptra, which is an open-source and vendor-agnostic silicon root of trust technology that supports remote attestation to mathematically verify device identities and prevent firmware tampering.

With mass volume production already underway, the 6th Gen AMD EPYC Embedded 9006 Series SP7 processors are expected to be shipped to customers later this month, the company said. Customers will be able to choose from configurations ranging from 64 to 256 cores, with the first 128-core offering launching on Nov. 15.

The Clearest Sign Of The Winter Blues

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Energy, mood or sleep lost to worry? A UK survey found a clearer winter pattern for one of the three.

Energy, mood or sleep lost to worry? A UK survey found a clearer winter pattern for one of the three.

Low energy is the clearest sign of the winter blues among three symptoms studied in young people in the UK, ahead of low mood and losing sleep over worry.

Low mood is also more pronounced in winter, although the difference is smaller.

Shorter days may play a part by shifting the body clock and affecting when young people feel alert, while changes in exercise and social routines could also contribute.

The researchers analysed almost 6,000 interviews with over 4,000 people aged 16 to 24 who took part in the UK’s Understanding Society survey.

They compared interviews conducted in December, January and February with those conducted during the rest of the year, using data from 2022 and 2023 for the main analysis.

On a 0–100 scale, the average score for low energy was about 3 points higher in winter, while the score for low mood was about 2 points higher.

The estimate for sleep lost through worry was smaller and compatible with no seasonal difference.

The figures describe differences between survey reports collected at different times of year, so they give a picture of the group average rather than trace how any one person’s symptoms changed.

Earlier Canadian survey research found that a wider range of depressive symptoms varied by season among young people than among older adults (Lukmanji et al., 2020).

Sleep schedules continue to shift through adolescence and into early adulthood, so the timing of daylight may influence when young people feel most alert.

Winter can also change the shape of an ordinary week, with fewer chances to be active outdoors or spend time with friends in person.

Feeling short of energy could make it harder to stay engaged through a day of lessons or a long shift, even when the person’s mood has barely changed.

The study was published in the journal PLOS ONE (Guan et al., 2026).

Psychologist, Jeremy Dean, PhD is the founder and author of PsyBlog. He holds a doctorate in psychology from University College London and two other advanced degrees in psychology. He has been writing about scientific research on PsyBlog since 2004. View all posts by Dr Jeremy Dean

New Manchester United manager hint dropped amid 'Mauricio Pochettino U-turn'

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Manchester United could well be moving towards appointing a new manager.

The Red Devils look set to part company with under-fire head coach Michael Carrick this week, according to widespread reports, with a crisis meeting set to be held in Monaco on Wednesday with club executives including Sir Jim Ratcliffe, Omar Berrada and Jason Wilcox.

Carrick’s position has come under severe scrutiny after just one win in 11 games in all competitions, and United were almost embarrassed at home against Aston Villa at the weekend, falling behind by two goals twice inside the opening minutes of either half.

It was a game that they roared back to draw, with Patrick Dorgu notching an injury-time equaliser, but the level of the overall performance was again cause for concern, with the 20-time Premier League champions mired in the relegation zone after 10 matches.

A three-strong shortlist looks to have come to the fore in terms of replacements for Carrick, with former Newcastle boss Eddie Howe, ex-Bayern Munich and Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann, and Mauricio Pochettino, currently the head coach of the USA national team, all considered.

Pochettino, formerly of Southampton, Tottenham and Chelsea, has been linked with the Old Trafford hotseat before, and has emerged as a wildcard candidate after not featuring in early reporting of potential managerial targets.

Now, however, in an update given by David Ornstein on The Athletic FC podcast, it appears that the Argentine could well be United’s preferred option, should they be willing to meet the $10million release clause that the former defender has in his USMNT contract.

“Julian Nagelsmann is a name that has been mentioned, but I don’t hear that he’s one Manchester United are looking to pursue,” Ornstein said on Tuesday.

“Eddie Howe has been heavily rumoured, but I don’t hear that he’s one they’re looking to pursue. So, by the process of elimination, you get to someone like Mauricio Pochettino."

Pochettino, now 54, has been previously considered by Ineos for a couple of positions, firstly at Nice, and more recently with United.

“In 2024, When [Erik] ten Hag’s job was on the line, United spoke to Pochettino,” Ornstein continued.

Legend Of Zelda: Tears Of The Kingdom Successor Officially Teased By Nintendo

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom was the last mainline entry in the series. Since its release, fans have been wondering what is next for the legendary franchise, beyond, of course, the recent spin-offs and remake. While it certainly feels as if Nintendo is gearing up for a future of ports and remakes, at least for the time being, it also clearly has its attention focused on the next big game, the one to dethrone Breath of the Wild and Tears of the Kingdom and flourish in a way remakes simply cannot.

Fortunately, among many discussions about the future of the Zelda franchise, Nintendo has officially teased the kind of experience fans can expect after Tears of the Kingdom. This wasn't merely a conversation about the next Zelda game, whatever that may be, but the next mainline title, the big-budget game that sees Nintendo throw everything at the wall and attempt to push the series forward, much in the same way it did with Breath of the Wild nearly a decade ago.

With new TotK projects still releasing today, it is no surprise that Nintendo hasn't quite moved on from the Wilds era or indeed announced the next mainline game. However, a recent interview with The Legend of Zelda producer Eiji Aonuma revealed that the next mainline Legend of Zelda game won't be dictated by the same creatives who've been shepherding the series thus far. Rather, Aonuma stated that the next Zelda game will be created by the fresh-faced developers at Nintendo who've been helping with the production of Ocarina of Time's remake.

In a conversation with AFP after receiving the honor of being made a Knight of the Order of Arts and Letters in France, Aonuma discussed how the Ocarina of Time remake only came into being as a result of new, "younger" developers coming into the fray and helping to "adapt the game in the best possible way" and "make it fun once you have the controls in your hands." It is this same young talent that Aonuma imagines pushing the series forward with the next mainline game.

He expressed that he is "very interested to see what the younger staff will imagine" and that he would offer his guidance so that the team can "think together" about what the future of the series will look like. Of course, that isn't a very definitive statement on what the next mainline Legend of Zelda game will be, but it is at least confirmation that Nintendo and Aonuma specifically are looking to the younger generation to direct the course of the franchise and push it to bold new heights.

It remains to be seen if the Zelda series will return to the Wilds era or whether it'll stick with the more traditional gameplay model that the Ocarina of Time remake is doing a great job of repopularizing. However, regardless of which direction this new younger generation of developers takes, it will likely be a long wait until we hear anything definitive about the series' future or, indeed, what the next game after Tears of the Kingdom will even look like.

Chris Pratt's The Terminal List Is Officially Flipping Its Premise

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Warning: SPOILERS ahead for Savage Son by Jack Carr!Chris Pratt's The Terminal List will officially be flipping its premise on its head following the latest news about the franchise's next chapter. The Pratt-led series is the pinnacle of a budding Prime Video franchise, which is defined by various TV series in its larger universe. While The Terminal List focuses on Pratt's James Reece, a soldier seeking revenge for the deaths of his men and family, the prequel series The Terminal List: Dark Wolf offers an origin for Ben Edwards (Taylor Kitsch) and his development. It also acted as a lead-in to season 2 of the main show.

The Terminal List season 2 is the latest installment of the series, continuing Reece's story while also tying into ideas that were introduced in The Terminal List: Dark Wolf. The intersection between characters across The Terminal List universe helped ground all three existing seasons in the same world. It also left off on a number of cliffhangers that can help define the series moving forward. The Terminal List season 2 ends with a new beginning for Reece, a next chapter that's already been confirmed.

That chapter is Savage Son, a continuation of The Terminal List based on the third book in Jack Carr's series of the same name. Prime Video's Savage Son adaptation was confirmed in November 2026, with the upcoming installment serving as a continuation beyond season 2 of the main show. Pratt's Reece will continue to be the central character, and it's expected allies like Raife Hastings (Tom Hopper) will come back too. But it also means a changed dynamic for the protagonist in the story to come.

While Reece has hunted down antagonists across the first two seasons of The Terminal List, Savage Son flips this premise on its head by having Reece on the receiving end of the hunt. In the novel, Reece going to a ranch in Montana owned by the Hastings family to recover from his brain surgery. However, escaped Russian operative Oliver Grey ends up in charge of an operation under Ivan Zharkov, a criminal Russian nationalist. His task is simple: find and kill James Reece.

This causes Reece and his allies to be hunted by the Russians, morphing into another global event as the protagonist is forced to deal with becoming the target. This ropes in the Hastings family as well, who become noteworthy allies for him as the rest of the story plays out. Reece being the one hunted instead of the hunter is a major alteration from previous installments, while also delivering a story that directly follows the events of season 2 in the process.

Based on Savage Son, it seems like The Terminal List's future is going to be quite dark, should the series generally follow the events of the novel. Reece's targeting puts the lives of those he cares about in danger, with some people not making it out of the conflict alive. It also lays the groundwork for his story in novels afterward. There are particular ties to his father, Tom Reece, that haven't been fully explored either, which Savage Son does hint at during its story.

As for the larger future of The Terminal List, Savage Son is only the third in an eight-book series, meaning there's still plenty of stories for the franchise to cover going forward. But this next installment will most certainly be its most unique, as it proves that even Reece is capable of being hunted down, and isn't safe despite everything he's already had to endure.

Smash Tacos Are The Dinner Hack My Family Never Stops Requesting

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Whether I’m whipping up a quick weeknight dinner for one or feeding the whole family, smash tacos are always a good call. Featuring a layer of crispy meat pressed into a soft tortilla that’s topped with condiments of your choice, they’re a speedy, flexible dinner that readily adapts to whatever you have in your fridge.

While I have tons of experience with smash dumplings, I made my very first smash tacos while developing the recipe for chicken Caesar smash tacos and coming up with the concepts for meatball sub smash tacos and Philly cheesesteak smash tacos. As I quickly learned, smash tacos are pretty simple to make, but if you haven’t done it before, there are definitely a few things to know.

Here are a few tips to make your first experience making them just a little smoother:

Whether you’re mixing in a ton of spices or keeping it simple with just salt and pepper, it’s important to work the ground meat until it feels tacky; otherwise, the meat won’t hold together on the tortilla when you sear it, and you’ll end up with a fragmented patty instead of one smooth layer.

Even if you’re using 90% lean, beef shrinks when it cooks, so if your patty is beef, spread it to the very edge of the tortilla—maybe even a little over. That gives you the best chance of getting full coverage once it’s cooked. Chicken, on the other hand, doesn’t shrink much at all (in my experience), so in that case it’s fine to leave a little border around the edges.

Let the patties sear, undisturbed, for a solid two minutes before flipping them. This gives you the best shot at that caramelized crust, and that’s where all the flavor is!

A smash taco doesn’t always require cheese (see the gyro smash tacos below!), but if you’re going there, I highly recommend using the broiler to melt it. In my experience, Kraft singles are the only type of cheese that will melt completely before the bottom of the smash taco burns. It might be an extra step, but it’s worth it to avoid an acrid bottom.

A truly amazing smash taco needs a plethora of toppings, so don’t hold back here! Load it up. While the OG smash tacos were a mash-up of a smash burger and a taco, you don’t need to limit yourself to classic burger flavors here. Almost any sandwich can be hacked into the smash taco format, but as I’ve discovered, salads are also fair game for inspiration. Here are five of my favorite smash taco flavor profiles:

Taking inspiration from the granddaddy of all burgers, these smash burger tacos feature all the familiar fixings of a classic Big Mac, including American cheese, “shrettuce,” pickles, and Big Mac sauce. All they need is a side of fries.

When you’re craving a salad, but everyone else at the table insists they need “real” dinner, these chicken Caesar smash tacos are the perfect compromise. Ground chicken patties are topped with squiggles of Caesar dressing, a mound of romaine, crispy croutons, and tons of cheese for a dinner that manages to make even a taco feel healthy.

I love ordering a cheesesteak when I’m out, but if I’m craving one at home, these Philly cheesesteak tacos are my lazy go-to. Topped with sautéed peppers and onions and lots of melty provolone cheese, they’re the perfect quick weeknight meal—or late-night snack. And, unlike an actual cheesesteak, you can definitely eat more than one!

Gyros and tacos are iconic all on their own, but now there’s actual proof that they can become something even better when they’re combined. Loaded with shawarma-spiced chicken, cucumber, tomato, tzatziki, and (of course!) shoestring fries, these gyro smash tacos are the definition of over-the-top indulgence.

Forming and baking individual meatballs can take some time, but these smash tacos are the perfect shortcut. Topped with marinara, mozz, and plenty of Parm, they’re bringing all the saucy, cheesy comfort vibes of an actual meatball sub—just in a quicker, more convenient weeknight package. Fresh basil on top is nice if you have it. If you don’t, a little dried oregano also works!

Overwatch doesn’t have ‘any intention’ of making 6-vs-6 the main mode, but it’s now ‘a second among equals’

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The never-ending battle between Overwatch players choosing five-vs-five or six-vs-six will rage on.

In a new Director’s Take blog today, game director Aaron Keller confirmed that Overwatch will continue to support both formats, but with a renewed focus on the classic six-vs-six, which the studio is “starting to think of it as a second among equals.”

“We always say that we want to make the game you all want to play and it’s obvious that we have folks who want different things,” Keller said, citing player surveys that were very split between the two options, as seen above. “Over the past few years, we’ve realized that there doesn’t have to be a ‘winner’ between five-vs-five and six-vs-six. The real competition should be in your matches, not between two modes that can coexist. You can play either one (many people actually play both!) and we’re going to support that choice.”

Personally? I’m a fan of six-vs-six, but that’s because I’m a tank main. Solo tanking is a pain compared to having a buddy on the frontline with you, and I can see that there are many who agree with me…but also many who don’t. Overwatch will continue to feature both, Keller says, and there won’t be any radical switches soon either, other than an additional six-vs-six queue.

“Six-vs-six has been slowly growing in popularity over the last few years; however, the five-vs-five version of Open Queue was popular too,” Keller said. “We’d like to learn how many people are playing Open Queue six-vs-six for the role flexibility and how many people are playing it for the team size. Running both modes at the same time should produce some good data points to show us where we ought to be putting more energy.”

Keller warned that adding the new queue (Role Queue six-vs-six) instead of replacing an old one may impact queue times, but it “should be better overall for our community.” He also said that the team theorizes that some matchmaking changes coming in season six “will keep queue times more manageable” alongside the new option.

“We’ll put more energy into balance patches for [six-vs-six] and we’ll continue to look for the right format that really gives our community the experience they’re looking for,” Keller said. “Whether that’s Open Queue, Role Queue, a combination of both, or a new format that we’ve yet to try out, we’re here to figure out what works best for you.”

AMD Zen 6 EPYC ‘Venice’ deep dive and benchmarking

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
